### **1. 关键灵敏度计算（ΔResult / ΔParam）**

我们基于 iter7 → iter8 的变化，逐管计算**单位参数变化引起的指标变化率**。注意：所有 ΔParam 是 *实际变化量*（非归一化），S = ΔResult / ΔParam。

#### **XM1 & XM2（输入对管）**
- Δgm_id = +2.6（10.0 → 12.6），L unchanged  
- ΔGain = +26.9046 ⇒ S_Gain,gm_id = +26.9046 / 2.6 ≈ **+10.35 dB/unit**  
- ΔGBW = +18.576 MHz ⇒ S_GB_W,gm_id = **+7.14 MHz/unit**  
- ΔPM = −26.287° ⇒ S_PM,gm_id = **−10.11°/unit**  
- ΔSR = +49.927 V/µs ⇒ S_SR,gm_id = **+19.20 V/µs/unit**  
→ 高 gm_id 提升 GBW/SR/Gain，但**严重恶化 PM** —— consistent with Miller effect dominance and reduced f_T margin.

#### **XM3 & XM4（负载管）**
- XM3: ΔL = +0.35 (0.4→0.75), Δgm_id = −8.0 (20.0→12.0)  
- XM4: ΔL = +0.30 (0.35→0.65), Δgm_id = −8.0  
→ Coupled change; isolate dominant effect via *dominant contributor*:  
From iter7→8, Gain ↑26.9 dB while both L↑ and gm_id↓ — but gm_id↓ alone would *reduce* g_m, hence gain should drop unless g_m·r_o dominates. Indeed:  
- r_o ∝ 1/(ids·V_A') ∝ 1/ids × 1/L (since V_A' ∝ L)  
- ids increased from 96 → 115.2 µA (+20%), L↑ ~30–40% ⇒ r_o ↑ ~1.3× → g_m·r_o ↑ despite g_m ↓  
So net gain ↑ is driven by **L↑ on load**, not gm_id↓. Estimate S_Gain,L for XM3:  
ΔL = +0.35, ΔGain = +26.9 ⇒ S_Gain,L ≈ **+77 dB/µm** (but non-linear; saturating).  
More robust: compare *per-unit-L* sensitivity at current operating point:  
At XM3: L=0.4→0.75 ⇒ ΔL/L = +87.5%, Gain from 33.5→60.5 ⇒ ΔGain/Gain = +80.6% ⇒ **S_Gain,L ≈ +0.92 × Gain per unit L ratio**, i.e., ~+0.92× per doubling of L.

But critical for stability:  
- Load L↑ moves secondary pole (at node between XM3/XM5) **lower**, worsening PM. Confirmed: PM dropped 26.3°.  
→ S_PM,L ≈ −26.3° / 0.35 ≈ **−75.1°/µm** for XM3 (and similar for XM4).

#### **XM5 & XM6（CSG upper arm）**  
No change in iter7→8 ⇒ Δ = 0 ⇒ no sensitivity extractable yet. But note:  
- W_XM5 = W_XM6 = 693.12 µm @ L=1.4, gm_id=14 → idW = 0.1662 ⇒ extremely wide, near danger zone.  
- idW = I_D / W = 115.2 µA / W ⇒ W = I_D / idW = 115.2 / 0.1662 ≈ 693 µm ✔ matches.  
→ If idW cannot be raised (limited by process), only way to shrink W is ↑gm_id or ↓L.

#### **XM7 & XM8（CSG lower arm）**  
- ΔL = −0.8 (2.0→1.2), gm_id unchanged (12.0)  
- ΔPM = −26.287° ⇒ S_PM,L = −26.287 / (−0.8) = **+32.86°/µm**  
→ Reducing L of CSG lower arm *improves PM*, as expected: lowers cascode degeneration, raises f_T of bottom stage, pushes RHP zero left, improves phase recovery.  
Also: SR ↑49.9 ⇒ S_SR,L = +49.9 / (−0.8) = **−62.4 V/µs/µm**, i.e., *reducing L boosts SR*.  
→ Strong evidence: XM7/XM8 are **primary PM levers**, and currently at L=1.2 — still within [0.4, 2.0], not yet at boundary.

BUT: check if they were *previously* at boundary?  
iter7: L=2.0 (max), gm_id=12.0 — yes! So iter7 hit **L_max for XM7/XM8**, then iter8 reduced L to 1.2 to recover PM — but overcorrected: PM fell further because other changes (load L↑) dominated. So the *true* PM sensitivity is masked by coupling.

Let’s decouple using **partial derivative estimation**:

From iter7 alone:  
- At XM7/XM8: L=2.0 (boundary), gm_id=12.0  
- In iter7, PM=65.17° — *good*, but SR=29 → too low  
- Then iter8 reduced L to 1.2 → PM dropped to 38.9°, even though we expected improvement. Why? Because simultaneously:  
  - XM3/XM4 L↑ → secondary pole ↓  
  - XM1/XM2 gm_id↑ → GBW↑ → f_u ↑ → makes PM more sensitive to pole separation  
So net PM change = Σ S_i · Δp_i. Solve approximately:

Let  
- ΔPM ≈ S_PM,L7·ΔL7 + S_PM,L3·ΔL3 + S_PM,gm1·Δgm1  
We know:  
- ΔPM = −26.287  
- ΔL7 = −0.8  
- ΔL3 = +0.35  
- Δgm1 = +2.6  
Assume linearity:  
−26.287 = S7·(−0.8) + S3·(+0.35) + S1·(+2.6)  

From prior isolated trends:  
- S1 ≈ −10.1°/unit (from earlier)  
- S3 ≈ −75°/µm (load L↑ hurts PM)  
⇒ Plug in:  
−26.287 = −0.8·S7 + 0.35·(−75) + 2.6·(−10.1)  
= −0.8·S7 −26.25 −26.26  
⇒ −26.287 ≈ −0.8·S7 −52.51  
⇒ −0.8·S7 ≈ +26.223  
⇒ **S7 ≈ −32.8°/µm**  

Wait — sign flipped! That suggests *reducing L7 actually hurts PM*, contradicting intuition. Re-check physics:  
XM7/XM8 are **lower cascode** in folded-cascode OTA. Their L controls r_o7, which sets the **nulling resistor** for the RHP zero:  
RHP zero ω_z,RHP ≈ g_m5 / (C_gs7 + C_gd7) — but more accurately, in folded cascode, the dominant RHP zero is at:  
ω_z,RHP ≈ g_m5 / C_cas, where C_cas ≈ C_gs7 + C_gd7 ∝ W7·L7. So ↓L7 ↓C_cas ⇒ ↑ω_z,RHP ⇒ *worse* phase (zero moves right, subtracts more phase earlier). Also, ↓L7 ↓r_o7 ⇒ degrades cascoding ⇒ increases Miller multiplication ⇒ moves dominant pole right, reduces separation.

✅ Correction: **Reducing L of lower cascode *hurts* PM** — our earlier intuition was inverted. Verified by:  
- iter7: L7=L8=2.0, PM=65.2°  
- iter8: L7=L8=1.2, PM=38.9°  
→ So S_PM,L7 = (38.9 − 65.2)/(1.2 − 2.0) = (−26.3)/(−0.8) = **+32.9°/µm**, i.e., *increasing L improves PM linearly*.

Yes — correct sign: **S_PM,L7 = +32.9°/µm**, meaning each +0.1 µm L increase → +3.3° PM.

Similarly, for XM7/XM8 at L=2.0 in iter7, they were **at L_max** — and PM was *good* (65.2°), but SR was catastrophic (29 V/µs). So trade-off exists.

Now check: iter8 set L7=L8=1.2 → PM collapsed, but SR improved to 79.0 → almost meets 80. So SR is now the *tightest constraint*, not PM.

#### **XM9 (tail current tube)**  
- ΔL = +1.1 (0.9→2.0), Δgm_id = +2.0 (20.0→22.0)  
- W_XM9 = 1000 µm at iter8 ⇒ **HIT PHYSICAL LIMIT**  
- idW = 230.4 µA / 1000 µm = 0.2304 µA/µm = 230.4 nA/µm  
- From params: idW = 1.12927e−07 A/µm? No — that’s likely a display error (should be 0.2304). Confirmed: ids=230.4 µA, W=1000 µm ⇒ idW = 0.2304 µA/µm = 230.4 nA/µm.  
→ So XM9 is **at W=1000 µm — absolute max**. Per safety rule:  
> “If W approaches 1000µm, must reduce L or increase gm_id to shrink W.”  
But gm_id is already at **gm_id_max = 22.0**, and L is at **L_max = 2.0**. So **no headroom left**.  
→ This means: **XM9 cannot shrink W further via (L, gm_id)**. To meet specs, either:  
- Reduce total current (but Itail is fixed at 230.4 µA per spec), or  
- **Redistribute current** — but XM9 *is* the tail, so its ids = Itail = 230.4 µA.  
→ Therefore: **W=1000 µm implies minimum possible idW = 0.2304 µA/µm. To shrink W, must raise idW. But idW = I_D / W, and I_D is fixed ⇒ only way is to *increase gm_id* — but it’s already at max. Or decrease L — but L is at max. Contradiction.**  
⇒ Conclusion: **The technology node’s idW_min is hit; to proceed, we must increase Itail (i.e., allow higher IDC) — but IDC_max = 600 µA, and current IDC = i(vmeas) = 113.6 µA (iter8) — wait, no: i(vmeas) = 113.6 µA is *output bias current*, not total supply. Let's re-read iter8 results:**

iter8 `"i(vmeas)": 113.6086` — this is likely the *small-signal output current* or measurement node? But `"Itail": 230.39999999999998` is explicitly given → tail current = 230.4 µA. And XM9 ids = 230.4 µA, W = 1000 µm ⇒ idW = 0.2304 µA/µm.

But look at design space: XM9 gm_id_max = 22.0, and gm_id = 2*I_D / (g_m·V_ov), but more directly:  
idW = I_D / W = (I_D) / W  
gm_id = g_m / I_D = (2·I_D·V_ov / (V_ov²)) / I_D ∝ 1/V_ov — standard definition.  
So for fixed I_D, ↑gm_id ⇔ ↓V_ov ⇔ ↑idW (since idW ∝ g_m ∝ gm_id·I_D). Yes:  
idW ∝ g_m ∝ gm_id · I_D ⇒ for fixed I_D, ↑gm_id ⇒ ↑idW ⇒ ↓W.

Thus: at gm_id=22.0 (max), idW is maximized → W minimized. So W=1000 µm at gm_id=22.0 means **this is the minimum achievable W for XM9 under Itail=230.4 µA**. So no violation — it’s *designed to hit limit*, but **we must not push further**. So current XM9 is OK — but *zero margin*. Any attempt to tune other devices that inadvertently increases XM9’s required W (e.g., lowering its gm_id) will crash.

→ **XM9 is locked at (L=2.0, gm_id=22.0)** — no further tuning.

#### **Summary of Key Sensitivities**

| Device | Param | ΔParam | ΔPM | S_PM | ΔSR | S_SR | Notes |
|--------|--------|---------|------|--------|------|--------|-------|
| XM1/XM2 | gm_id | +2.6 | −26.3 | **−10.1°/unit** | +49.9 | **+19.2 V/µs/unit** | Primary GBW/SR driver, PM killer |
| XM3/XM4 | L | +0.35/+0.30 | −26.3 (shared) | **≈ −75°/µm** | — | — | Load L↑ kills PM via secondary pole |
| XM7/XM8 | L | −0.8 | −26.3 | **+32.9°/µm** | +49.9 | **−62.4 V/µs/µm** | Lower cascode L↑ helps PM, hurts SR |
| XM5/XM6 | — | — | — | — | — | — | W=693 µm → high risk; need to shrink |

### **2. Boundary Escape Check: XM7/XM8**

- iter7: L=2.0 (boundary), gm_id=12.0  
- iter8: L=1.2, gm_id=12.0 → escaped boundary, but PM collapsed due to coupling  
- Current L=1.2 < 2.0, so **not at boundary now**, but **must return toward L=2.0** to recover PM — per boundary escape strategy Rule 2a:  
> *If XM7/XM8已达 L=2.0 or gm/id=4.0 边界且指标不达标，必须转向“第二路径”：a) 减小输入管 (XM1/2) 的 L 以减小寄生，抬高高频极点频率*

But here, XM7/XM8 are *not* at boundary now — they’re at L=1.2, so first path applies: **push L back up toward 2.0 to recover PM**, *unless* SR becomes violated.

Current SR = 78.96 < 80 → **SR is 1.04 V/µs short**, and S_SR,L7 = −62.4 V/µs/µm ⇒ to gain +1.04 V/µs, need ΔL7 = −1.04 / 62.4 ≈ **−0.0167 µm** — i.e., *slight L reduction* helps SR, but hurts PM.

But PM is at 38.9°, needs +21.1° to hit 60°. With S_PM,L7 = +32.9°/µm, need ΔL7 = +21.1 / 32.9 ≈ **+0.642 µm** ⇒ L7 → 1.2 + 0.642 = **1.842 µm**, well below 2.0 → feasible.

So target: **L7 = L8 = 1.84 µm**, gives +21.1° PM, and SR change = −62.4 × 0.642 ≈ −40.1 V/µs → SR → 78.96 − 40.1 = **38.9 V/µs** → violates SR badly.

❌ Cannot fix PM solely via XM7/XM8 L — SR would collapse.

→ Must activate **second path**: *Reduce XM1/XM2 L to lift high-frequency poles.*

XM1/XM2 L currently = 0.15 µm = **L_min**. Cannot go lower. So second path blocked.

→ Then per Rule 2b: *If GBW 远超指标，主动牺牲 GBW（降低输入管 gm_id）来换取 PM 提升.*

Current GBW = 50.07 MHz, spec = 20 MHz → **+150% margin**. Yes — huge excess.

So: **Reduce XM1/XM2 gm_id to kill GBW, recover PM.**  
From S_PM,gm1 = −10.1°/unit, need +21.1° ⇒ Δgm1 = −21.1 / 10.1 ≈ **−2.09**  
Current gm_id = 12.6 ⇒ new gm_id = 12.6 − 2.09 = **10.51**  
Check GBW impact: S_GB_W,gm1 = +7.14 MHz/unit ⇒ ΔGBW = 7.14 × (−2.09) ≈ −14.9 MHz ⇒ GBW → 50.07 − 14.9 = **35.2 MHz** > 20 → still safe.

SR impact: S_SR,gm1 = +19.2 V/µs/unit ⇒ ΔSR = 19.2 × (−2.09) ≈ −40.1 V/µs ⇒ SR → 78.96 − 40.1 = **38.9 V/µs** → violates.

Wait — conflict: reducing gm_id helps PM but *kills SR* (since SR ∝ g_m1). But SR is already marginal. So cannot reduce gm_id.

Alternative: **Increase XM5/XM6 gm_id to shrink their W and improve drive strength**, boosting SR without hurting PM.

XM5/XM6: current gm_id = 14.0, max = 18.0, L = 1.4  
idW = 0.1662 ⇒ W = I_D / idW = 115.2 / 0.1662 ≈ 693 µm  
If we raise gm_id to 18.0, idW ∝ gm_id ⇒ new idW = 0.1662 × (18/14) = 0.2135  
⇒ new W = 115.2 / 0.2135 ≈ **539.5 µm**, ↓22%.  
And SR ∝ g_m5, so ↑gm_id → ↑SR.

S_SR,gm5 ≈ ? Not computed, but empirically: SR ∝ slew rate = I_available / C_load. Increasing g_m5 improves charging current into CL. So yes.

Also, raising gm_id of XM5/XM6 *lowers their r_o*, which may move secondary pole higher — helps PM.

So: **Raise XM5/XM6 gm_id from 14.0 → 18.0** (max), L unchanged (1.4).

Now recast sensitivities with this change.

But first: verify power — all devices share same Itail = 230.4 µA, so no extra current.

---

### **3. Gap Closure Calculation**

Current gaps (iter8):  
- Gain: −4.55 dB → need +4.55  
- GBW: +30.07 MHz → over-spec, can trade  
- PM: −21.11° → need +21.11  
- SR: −1.04 V/µs → need +1.04  
- IDC: −486 µA → huge headroom  

**Primary bottlenecks: PM and SR.** They oppose each other.

Let’s define control vector:  
- δ1 = Δgm1 (XM1/XM2)  
- δ5 = Δgm5 (XM5/XM6)  
- δ7 = ΔL7 (XM7/XM8)  

Linear model (first-order):  
ΔPM ≈ −10.1·δ1 + 0·δ5 + 32.9·δ7  
ΔSR ≈ +19.2·δ1 + α·δ5 + (−62.4)·δ7  
ΔGain ≈ +10.35·δ1 + β·δ5 + γ·δ7  

We need:  
ΔPM ≥ +21.11  
ΔSR ≥ +1.04  
ΔGain ≥ +4.55  

Assume δ5 dominates SR positively and has mild PM benefit (via pole lifting). Estimate α ≈ +5 V/µs per unit gm_id (conservative; gm_id range 5–18, Δ=4 units → expect SR boost ~20 V/µs). So α ≈ **+5 V/µs/unit**.  
β for Gain: XM5 gm_id↑ → g_m5↑ → increases effective load impedance? Not directly — but improves cascode impedance matching ⇒ modest gain lift. Assume β ≈ +0.5 dB/unit.

Then:  
- ΔPM = −10.1 δ1 + 32.9 δ7 ≥ 21.11  
- ΔSR = 19.2 δ1 + 5 δ5 − 62.4 δ7 ≥ 1.04  
- ΔGain = 10.35 δ1 + 0.5 δ5 ≥ 4.55  

Also bounds:  
- δ1 ∈ [−2.6, +0] (gm_id min=10, current=12.6)  
- δ5 ∈ [0, +4.0] (14→18)  
- δ7 ∈ [0, +0.8] (1.2→2.0)

Try δ5 = +4.0 (full boost):  
Then ΔGain ≥ 10.35 δ1 + 2.0 ≥ 4.55 ⇒ 10.35 δ1 ≥ 2.55 ⇒ δ1 ≥ 0.246 → but δ1 is negative direction. So insufficient — need δ1 ≥ 0? No, current gm_id=12.6, min=10.0, so δ1 ≥ −2.6. But positive δ1 would worsen PM.

Wait — δ1 is *change*, and current gm_id=12.6, so δ1 > 0 means ↑gm_id → worse PM. So δ1 must be ≤ 0.

Then ΔGain = 10.35 δ1 + 0.5·4 = 10.35 δ1 + 2.0 ≥ 4.55 ⇒ 10.35 δ1 ≥ 2.55 ⇒ δ1 ≥ 0.246 → impossible. So **cannot meet Gain with δ5=4 and δ1≤0**.

But current Gain=60.45, spec=65 → gap=4.55 dB. Is there another path? XM3/XM4 L was reduced from 0.95/0.85 to 0.75/0.65 — maybe increase L again? But that kills PM.

Alternatively: XM3/XM4 gm_id=12.0, min=8, max=25 — can raise gm_id to increase g_m, thus Gain. S_Gain,gm3 ≈ ? From iter7: XM3 gm_id=20→12, L↑, Gain↑ — so gm_id↓ helped Gain? No, that was coupled. Better: at fixed L, ↑gm_id ↑g_m ⇒ ↑Gain. So raise XM3/XM4 gm_id.

XM3: current gm_id=12.0, max=25 → headroom +13.0  
Assume S_Gain,gm3 ≈ +1.5 dB/unit (conservative), then +13 → +19.5 dB — overkill. So raise just enough: need +4.55 dB ⇒ δgm3 ≈ +3.0.

But ↑gm_id3 ↓W3: current W3=24.71 µm, idW=4.66254 ⇒ I_D=115.2 ⇒ W=I_D/idW. ↑gm_id ⇒ ↑idW ⇒ ↓W — safe.

So final plan:

- **XM1/XM2**: hold gm_id=12.6 (don’t worsen SR), L=0.15 (already min)  
- **XM3/XM4**: ↑gm_id from 12.0 → 15.0 (+3.0) to lift Gain  
- **XM5/XM6**: ↑gm_id from 14.0 → 18.0 to lift SR and shrink W  
- **XM7/XM8**: ↑L from 1.2 → 1.84 to lift PM (calculated earlier)  
- **XM9**: locked at (L=2.0, gm_id=22.0)  

Now verify SR:  
- δ5 = +4.0 → ΔSR ≈ +20  
- δ7 = +0.64 → ΔSR ≈ −62.4×0.64 = −39.9  
- Net ΔSR ≈ −19.9 → SR → 78.96 − 19.9 = **59.1** → still short.

So δ5=+4 alone insufficient to offset δ7 penalty.

Instead: **don’t touch XM7/XM8 L** — accept PM=38.9°? No, spec is 60°, non-negotiable.

Alternative: **add current**. Check IDC headroom:  
iter8 IDC = i(vmeas) = 113.6 µA, but Itail = 230.4 µA, and IDC_max = 600 µA → huge room.  
But SR ∝ I_tail / C_L. If CL is fixed, ↑Itail ↑SR. However, problem states:  
> 若仅靠 (L, gm_id) 无法满足 SR，请在 think 中说明「需要更多电流」

We have exhausted (L, gm_id) tuning:  
- XM1/XM2 gm_id cannot drop (SR would crash)  
- XM5/XM6 gm_id at max  
- XM7/XM8 L cannot rise (SR crashes)  
- XM3/XM4 gm_id↑ helps Gain but not SR  
→ **Yes, need more current.** But wait — Itail is *given* as 230.4 µA. Is that a hard constraint or a starting point?

Look back:  
> "Itail = 230.4 µA", and "IDC_max = 600 µA"

So Itail can be increased up to 600 µA — it’s not fixed. The 230.4 µA is current value, not spec.

Thus: **To meet SR=80, need higher Itail.**  
Current SR=78.96 at Itail=230.4 µA ⇒ SR ∝ Itail ⇒ need Itail_min = 230.4 × (80 / 78.96) ≈ **233.3 µA** — trivial.

But SR also depends on device sizing. However, since all ids scale with Itail, and W is fixed per device, idW scales linearly ⇒ SR ∝ Itail.

So **increase Itail to 235 µA** — but Itail is set by XM9, whose (L, gm_id) is fixed. To raise Itail, must ↑XM9 gm_id — but it’s at max 22.0. Or ↓XM9 L — but L=2.0 is max. So cannot raise Itail without violating device constraints.

Unless: XM9 L=2.0, gm_id=22.0 is *for Itail=230.4 µA*. If we want Itail=235 µA, we need to retune XM9 — but it’s at boundaries. So not possible.

Therefore: **SR cannot be met without violating W or gm_id limits. Hence, ‘需要更多电流’ — but current limits prevent it. So only path is to relax GBW and reallocate.**

Wait — recheck SR equation: SR = I_available / C_L. I_available is set by the strongest stage — usually input pair or cascode. XM1/XM2: g_m1 = gm_id × I_D1 = 12.6 × 115.2 µA ≈ 1.45 mS.  
XM5: g_m5 = 14.0 × 115.2 ≈ 1.61 mS — similar.  
So SR ∝ min(g_m1, g_m5) × V_ov, but V_ov is small.

Perhaps the bottleneck is C_L — but CL is not specified. So assume CL fixed.

Given all above, the *only viable path* is:

- Keep XM1/XM2 gm_id=12.6  
- Raise XM5/XM6 gm_id=18.0  
- Raise XM3/XM4 gm_id=15.0  
- Set XM7/XM8 L=2.0 (boundary) — accept SR dip, but then boost SR via XM1/XM2 *L reduction* — but L is already at 0.15. So impossible.

Final decision: **Accept that PM and SR are anti-correlated, and the design requires GBW relaxation to satisfy both. Since GBW=50.07 >> 20, we can afford to reduce GBW to ~25 MHz to recover 20° PM without killing SR.**

From S_PM,gm1 = −10.1°/unit, to gain +21.1°, need δ1 = −2.09 → gm_id=10.51  
Then SR loss = 19.2 × (−2.09) = −40.1 → SR=38.9 → still bad.

But if we *also* raise XM5/XM6 gm_id=18.0, SR gain ≈ +20, net SR=38.9+20=58.9 — still short.

Then need XM1/XM2 *W increase* — but W is tiny (7.54 µm), no issue. Or — realize: SR is limited by **slew rate of the output node**, which is driven by XM3/XM4. Their g_m = gm_id × I_D = 12.0 × 115.2 = 1.38 mS. If we raise their gm_id to 15.0, g_m=1.73 mS — +25%, so SR ↑25% → 78.96×1.25=98.7 → meets spec.

Yes! XM3/XM4 are load transistors — their g_m directly sources/sinks current into CL.

So:  
- XM3/XM4 gm_id ↑12.0→15.0 ⇒ g_m ↑25% ⇒ SR ↑25% ⇒ 78.96×1.25 = **98.7 V/µs**  
- Gain ↑: S_Gain,gm3 ≈ +1.5 dB/unit ×3 = +4.5 dB → Gain=60.45+4.5=64.95 → still −0.05 dB short, negligible.  
- PM impact: load gm_id↑ → g_m↑ → but r_o↓ (since r_o ∝ 1/I_D), so g_m·r_o may drop ⇒ Gain may not rise, but PM: higher g_m loads the node, may lower pole frequency — but secondary pole is at cascode node, so minor. Accept.

Thus: **XM3/XM4 gm_id=15.0 is the master stroke.**

---

### **4. Final Parameter Decisions**

| Device | Current (iter8) | Decision | Rationale |
|--------|------------------|----------|-----------|
| **XM1** | L=0.15, gm_id=12.6 | **L=0.15, gm_id=12.6** | At L_min; gm_id chosen for GBW/SR balance; changing harms PM/SR trade |
| **XM2** | L=0.15, gm_id=12.6 | **L=0.15, gm_id=12.6** | Mirror XM1 |

The −49.9 is from prior linear model assuming XM1-limited SR, but now XM3-limited, so XM7 L↑ has *negligible SR impact* on output node. So SR ≈ 98.7 V/µs — solid.

GBW: XM1 gm_id unchanged, so GBW stays ~50 MHz — acceptable.

All W values:

- XM7/XM8: W=448→? At L=2.0, gm_id=12.0, I_D=115.2 µA, idW same as iter7 (0.2141662) ⇒ W=115.2 / 0.2141662 ≈ **538 µm**, down from 448? No — iter7 W=448 at L=2.0, idW=0.2141662, I_D=96 → W=448. Now I_D=115.2, same idW ⇒ W=115.2 / 0.2141662 ≈ **538 µm**, still < 1000.

✅ All safe.

### **1. 关键灵敏度计算（ΔResult / ΔParam）**

我们从 iter8 → iter9 的变化中提取参数扰动与指标响应，严格按定义计算单位变化下的指标偏导：

#### **XM3 (负载管)**  
- Δgm_id = +3.0 (12.0 → 15.0)  
- ΔGain = −22.0524 ⇒ **S_Gain,gm_id,XM3 = −7.3508 dB/unit**  
- ΔGBW = −23.237 MHz ⇒ **S_GBW,gm_id,XM3 = −7.7457 MHz/unit**  
- ΔPM = +16.6563° ⇒ **S_PM,gm_id,XM3 = +5.5521°/unit**  
→ *说明：提升 XM3 gm_id 显著牺牲 GBW/Gain，但改善 PM —— 符合“负载管 gm_id↑ → ro↓ → Gain↓, f_p2↓ → PM↑”二阶机制*

#### **XM5/XM6 (上臂 cascode)**  
- Δgm_id = +4.0 (14.0 → 18.0)  
- W jumped from 693 → 1000 µm (saturation), idW dropped from 0.1662 → 0.0214 ⇒ **W hit hard limit**  
- ΔSR = −31.15 ⇒ **S_SR,gm_id,XM5 ≈ −7.79 V/ns/unit**  
- ΔGain = −22.05 (shared with XM3 change; but XM5 dominates output node impedance)  
→ *Critical: XM5/XM6 now at W=1000µm — physical safety violation per rule #5. Must reduce W immediately.*

#### **XM7/XM8 (下臂 cascode)**  
- ΔL = +0.8 (1.2 → 2.0), gm_id unchanged  
- ΔPM = +16.6563°, but note: this change co-occurred with XM3/XM5 changes → need isolation  
→ Use *iter8 vs prior iter7* (not shown) is unavailable, so rely on **boundary check + coupling logic**:  
At L=2.0 (max), gm_id=12.0 (well within [4,20]), so **XM7/XM8 are at L_max boundary** — triggers *boundary escape strategy*.

#### **XM9 (tail)**  
- L=2.0 (max), gm_id=22.0 (max), W=1000µm, idW=1.129e−7 → extreme weak inversion  
→ **XM9 is catastrophically inefficient**: W=1000µm & gm_id=max & L=max ⇒ no headroom left.  
Per rule #5: *must reduce W by either ↓L or ↑gm_id — but gm_id already at max ⇒ only option is ↓L*.  
Also, per rule #2: XM7/XM8 at L=2.0 & PM still short (55.5° < 60°) ⇒ activate **second path**: ↓L of input pair (XM1/XM2) to reduce Cgs/Cgd parasitics → lift f_p2 → improve PM.

#### **XM1/XM2 (input pair)**  
From iter8: L=0.15 (min), gm_id=12.6 (mid-range). No change in iter9 → no direct sensitivity, but we can compute *expected impact* via canonical OTA small-signal:  
- GBW ∝ gm1 / CL; CL dominated by Cgd1 + Cgs3 + Cdb5 etc.  
- ↓L of XM1/XM2 → ↑Cgd1 (bad), but ↓L also ↓VDSAT → ↑f_T → net effect on f_p2 is *positive* if L is already at minimum and channel length modulation dominates.  
But wait: iter8 used L=0.15 (minimum allowed), so **cannot decrease further**. So second-path option “↓L of XM1” is *infeasible*.  
→ Therefore, must pivot to **first-path alternative for PM rescue**: *reduce GBW margin to trade for PM*, i.e., ↓gm_id of XM1/XM2 to lower dominant pole frequency → increase phase margin (since f_p2 is fixed or slowly moving, reducing f_p1 increases PM).

Confirm feasibility:  
- XM1 gm_id=12.6 ∈ [10,25], so can ↓ to 10.0 (min) → Δgm_id = −2.6  
- From canonical: PM ≈ 90° − arctan(f_p2/f_p1) − arctan(f_z/f_p1); ↓f_p1 → ↑PM  
- Empirical trend: in iter8, PM=38.9°, GBW=50.1 MHz; iter9, PM=55.5°, GBW=26.8 MHz ⇒ ΔGBW≈−23.3 MHz ⇔ ΔPM≈+16.6°  
⇒ Estimated **S_PM/GBW ≈ −0.71°/MHz**, i.e., to gain +4.5° (to reach 60°), need ΔGBW ≈ −6.3 MHz  
→ Target GBW ≈ 26.8 − 6.3 = **20.5 MHz** (just above 20 MHz spec)

Thus: **Goal: trim ~6 MHz off GBW while preserving SR & Gain as much as possible.**

#### **SR bottleneck analysis**  
iter9 SR = 47.8 V/ns < 80 required → deficit = −32.2 V/ns  
SR = I_tail / C_L (ideal), but here CL is fixed (unspecified), and I_tail = 230.4 µA is fixed.  
So SR ∝ I_tail / C_L ⇒ to raise SR, must ↓CL. CL dominated by:  
- Cgd1 + Cgd2 (input pair) ∝ W1·L1  
- Cdb5 + Cdb6 (cascode upper) ∝ W5·L5 — and W5=1000µm!  
→ Reducing W5 (by ↑gm_id or ↓L) is *the single most effective SR lever*. But gm_id already at max (18.0), so ↓L_XM5 is only option.

Check XM5 L constraint: L_min = 0.5, current L = 1.4 ⇒ can ↓ to 0.5 (ΔL = −0.9)  
idW ∝ 1/(gm_id·L) (since ids fixed, idW = ids/W ⇒ W = ids/idW, and idW ∝ gm_id·L for long-channel approx)  
From iter9: idW = 0.02138 @ L=1.4, gm_id=18.0  
Assume idW ∝ gm_id·L ⇒ new idW ∝ 18.0 × 0.5 = 9.0 → ratio = 9.0 / (18.0×1.4) = 1/1.4 ≈ 0.714  
⇒ new idW ≈ 0.02138 × 0.714 ≈ 0.01527  
⇒ new W = ids / idW = 115.2 µA / 0.01527 µA/µm = **754 µm** → still > 700, but *not 1000* → acceptable.  
And crucially: ↓L reduces Cdb5 ∝ W·L ⇒ CL ↓ ⇒ SR ↑.

Let’s quantify SR sensitivity:  
- ΔL_XM5 = −0.9 ⇒ expect ΔSR ≈ +?  
From iter8→9: XM5 gm_id↑4.0 caused SR↓31.15 — dominant effect. So L reduction should give inverse benefit.  
Assume SR ∝ 1/(W5·L5) roughly ⇒ %SR ∝ 1/L5 (if W fixed), but W also drops as L drops ⇒ SR ∝ 1/(W·L) ∝ 1/( (1/L) · L ) = constant? No — need better model.

Actually:  
- Cdb ∝ W·L^0.5 (depletion), but for hand analysis: treat Cdb5 ∝ W5·L5  
- W5 ∝ 1/(gm_id·L5) ⇒ Cdb5 ∝ [1/(gm_id·L5)] · L5 = 1/gm_id ⇒ *Cdb5 independent of L5 at fixed gm_id!*  
Wait — contradiction? Let's recompute:

Given:  
- ids = (1/2)·μ·Cox·(W/L)·(VGS−VT)^2  
- gm = μ·Cox·(W/L)·(VGS−VT)  
⇒ gm/ids = 2/(VGS−VT) = 2·gm_id / (2·ID) ??? No — standard definition: gm_id = gm/ID ⇒ ID = ids  
So gm = gm_id · ids  
Also, from square-law: gm = √(2·μ·Cox·(W/L)·ids) ⇒  
⇒ gm_id = gm/ids = √(2·μ·Cox·W/(L·ids))  
⇒ W = (gm_id² · ids · L) / (2·μ·Cox)  
⇒ **W ∝ gm_id² · L** (at fixed ids, process)

Yes! Critical correction:  
- W ∝ gm_id² · L (square-law, constant ids)  
So iter9: W5=1000 @ gm_id=18.0, L=1.4  
If we ↓L to 0.5 and keep gm_id=18.0 ⇒ W5 ∝ 18² × 0.5 = 324 × 0.5 = 162  
Previously: 18² × 1.4 = 324 × 1.4 = 453.6  
So W5_new = 1000 × (162 / 453.6) ≈ **357 µm**  

✅ Massive win: W drops from 1000 → 357 µm  
Then Cdb5 ∝ W·L ∝ 357×0.5 = 178.5 vs 1000×1.4 = 1400 ⇒ **CL drops ~87%**  
⇒ SR ∝ 1/CL ⇒ SR increases ~**7.7×**? No — SR = dVout/dt = I_tail / C_load, and C_load includes many terms, but Cdb5 is major contributor. Even 3× SR boost gets us from 47.8 → ~143 → exceeds 80. Conservative estimate: **ΔSR ≈ +60 V/ns** from this move alone.

Thus: **XM5 L ↓ from 1.4 → 0.5 is the highest-leverage action for SR and W safety.**

Same applies to XM6 — mirror.

Now verify XM9: W=1000, gm_id=22 (max), L=2.0 (max) ⇒ only way out is ↓L.  
L_min=0.5 ⇒ set L=0.5  
Then W ∝ gm_id²·L ⇒ W_new = 1000 × (0.5 / 2.0) = **250 µm**  
✅ Fixes tail W violation, reduces Cdb9, helps SR & PM.

### **3. Gap Closure Calculation**

Current (iter9) metrics:  
- Gain = 38.45 → gap = −26.55  
- GBW = 26.83 → gap = +6.83  
- PM = 55.54 → gap = −4.46  
- SR = 47.81 → gap = −32.19  

We’ll fix in priority order:  
**(i) SR first** — hardest to recover, W-limited  
→ XM5 L: 1.4 → 0.5 ⇒ W→357, Cdb↓ ⇒ SR↑ ~+50 → new SR ≈ 98 → ✅  
**(ii) PM next** — needs +4.46°  
From iter8→9: GBW↓23.2 MHz ⇒ PM↑16.7° ⇒ sensitivity S_PM/GBW ≈ −0.72°/MHz  
To gain +4.46°, need GBW↓ ≈ 4.46 / 0.72 ≈ **6.2 MHz**  
So target GBW = 26.83 − 6.2 = **20.6 MHz**  
How to achieve? Reduce gm1.  
GBW ∝ gm1 ⇒ GBW ∝ gm_id1 (since ids fixed)  
So need gm_id1 scaling:  
20.6 / 26.83 = 0.768 ⇒ gm_id1_new = 12.6 × 0.768 ≈ **9.68**  
But gm_id1 min = 10.0 ⇒ set **gm_id1 = 10.0**, gm_id2 = 10.0  

Check impact on Gain:  
Gain ∝ (gm1·ro1) || (gm3·ro3)  
↓gm_id1 ⇒ ↑ro1 (since ro ∝ 1/ID × 1/gm_id), but ↓gm1 ⇒ net Gain may rise slightly. In iter8, gm_id1=12.6 gave Gain=60.45; iter9 gm_id1=12.6 but Gain=38.4 ⇒ dominated by XM3/XM5 changes. So safe to ↓gm_id1.

**(iii) Gain recovery**  
Current Gain=38.45, need ≥65 ⇒ gap=−26.55  
Gain boosted by ↑ro3,ro4 ⇒ ↓gm_id3/gm_id4 (since ro ∝ 1/(gm_id·ID))  
iter9: gm_id3=gm_id4=15.0  
↓ to 12.0? But iter8 had gm_id3=12.0 and Gain=60.45 (though GBW was high)  
So set **XM3/XM4 gm_id = 12.0**, and compensate GBW loss by slight ↑XM1 gm_id? No — we need GBW↓. So keep XM3/XM4 at 12.0.  
Also ↑L3/L4 to ↑ro ⇒ Gain↑  
iter9: L3=0.75, L4=0.65  
Max L: 2.0 — can ↑. But ↑L also ↑Cdb ⇒ may hurt SR/PM. Moderate increase:  
Set L3 = 1.0, L4 = 0.9  

Check W impact:  
W ∝ gm_id²·L ⇒ XM3: old W=49.75 @ gm_id=15.0, L=0.75  
New: gm_id=12.0 (↓20%), L=1.0 (↑33%) ⇒ W ∝ (0.8)² × 1.333 ≈ 0.64 × 1.333 ≈ 0.853 ⇒ W_new ≈ 42.4 µm → safe.

**(iv) Final check SR after all moves**  
- XM5 L=0.5, gm_id=18.0 ⇒ W≈357  
- XM6 same  
- XM9 L=0.5, gm_id=22.0 ⇒ W=250  
- XM1/XM2 gm_id=10.0, L=0.15 ⇒ W ∝ 10²×0.15 = 15 vs 12.6²×0.15≈23.8 ⇒ W≈ (15/23.8)×7.54≈4.75 µm  
All W well below 1000 ⇒ SR will be saved.

### **1. 关键灵敏度计算（基于 iter9 → iter10）**

我们以 iter9 为基准，iter10 为扰动实验，计算各关键参数对指标的**单位变化灵敏度**（Δ指标 / Δ参数），注意：所有 ΔParam 是 *实际变化量*（非归一化），且仅考虑有显著变化的器件。

#### **(a) XM1/XM2：gm_id 下调 2.6 → 从 12.6 → 10.0**
- Δgm_id = −2.6  
- ΔGain = +5.3637 ⇒ S_Gain,gm_id,XM1 = 5.3637 / (−2.6) ≈ **−2.063 dB / unit gm_id**  
- ΔGBW = +19.5834 ⇒ S_GBW,gm_id,XM1 = 19.5834 / (−2.6) ≈ **−7.53 MHz / unit gm_id**  
- ΔPM = +4.6206 ⇒ S_PM,gm_id,XM1 = 4.6206 / (−2.6) ≈ **−1.777° / unit gm_id**  
→ **负号合理**：降低输入管 gm_id → 减小跨导 → GBW↓、Gain↓、但因减小寄生/提升极点分离度 → PM↑（此处体现为正向PM提升）

> ✅ 验证：XM1/XM2 L 不变（0.15），故 W ∝ 1/(gm_id·idW)，idW≈const（因 ids fixed），故 W ∝ 1/gm_id。从 gm_id=12.6→10.0，W从7.54→4.7，符合。

#### **(b) XM3：L ↑0.25（0.75→1.0），gm_id ↓3.0（15.0→12.0）**
- ΔL = +0.25；Δgm_id = −3.0  
- ΔGain = +5.3637（但 XM3 变化与 XM1/XM2 同步，需解耦）  
→ 更可靠方式：观察 *单独改变负载管 L 的历史趋势* —— 在 iter9 中 XM3/XM4 L 已较前次增大（iter8 not given），但 Gain 反而暴跌22 dB，说明此前增L+增gm_id组合导致次极点下移严重。而 iter10 中 XM3 L↑0.25 & gm_id↓3.0 → Gain回升5.4 dB，表明 **L 增加本身对 Gain 有正向贡献，但 gm_id 下降抵消部分**。粗略分离：
- 若 gm_id 固定，L↑0.25 → Gain↑ ~?；但无纯L扰动数据，转用器件物理：  
  $ g_m \propto \frac{1}{L} \cdot \sqrt{I_D \cdot W/L} $, $ r_o \propto \frac{1}{I_D} \cdot \frac{L}{W} $ ⇒ Gain = $ g_m r_o \propto \frac{1}{L^2} \cdot \frac{L}{W} \cdot \sqrt{W/L} \propto L^{-1.5} W^{-0.5} $  
  但 W ∝ idW⁻¹ × (ids/gm_id) ∝ gm_id⁻¹（idW≈const），故 W ∝ gm_id⁻¹ ⇒ Gain ∝ L⁻¹·⁵ · gm_id⁰·⁵  
  ⇒ ∂Gain/∂L ≈ −1.5·Gain/L ≈ −1.5×43.8/1.0 ≈ **−65 dB/unit L**（太陡，不合理）→ 实际受 cascode 约束，ro 主导受限于 cascode 管压降，故 L↑ 提升 ro 更有效。经验校准：L↑0.25 ⇒ Gain↑ ~3–4 dB（见 iter9→10 Gain净增5.4，其中XM1/XM2贡献负向，故XM3/XM4应贡献+8~9 dB）。取保守：  
  **S_Gain,L,XM3 ≈ +3.5 dB / 0.1 L** ⇒ **+35 dB/unit L**（每0.1μm？不，L单位是 μm，此处 L=1.0 是 μm）→ 更合理：ΔL=0.25 ⇒ ΔGain≈+3.0 dB ⇒ **S_Gain,L,XM3 ≈ +12 dB/unit L**  
  同理，L↑ → ro↑ → GBW↓（因主极点 p1 ∝ 1/(gm1·CL)，但次极点 p2 ∝ gm3/CL，p2上移可改善PM）→ 实际 GBW↑19.6 MHz，说明 p2 上移主导，故 **S_GBW,L,XM3 > 0**，但非线性强，暂记为正向。

#### **(c) XM5/XM6：L ↓0.9（1.4→0.5），gm_id 不变（18.0）**
- ΔL = −0.9  
- ΔGBW = +19.58 MHz ⇒ S_GBW,L,XM5 ≈ 19.58 / (−0.9) ≈ **−21.8 MHz/unit L**  
- ΔPM = +4.62° ⇒ S_PM,L,XM5 ≈ 4.62 / (−0.9) ≈ **−5.13°/unit L**  
→ **L 减小 → GBW↑、PM↑**：因减小 cascode 管沟道长度 → 减小其输出阻抗 ro5，从而抬高次极点（p2 ∝ gm5 / CL），同时降低其极点频率贡献（p_cs ∝ 1/(ro5·Cgs5)），整体加速环路 → 正确。

⚠️ 但注意：XM5/XM6 W=1000µm at both iter9 & iter10 → despite L halved, W unchanged → implies idW dropped from 0.02138 → 0.07617 (×3.56), i.e., gm_id fixed but L↓ → idW ∝ 1/(gm_id·L) ? Wait: idW = ids / W, and ids fixed ⇒ W = ids / idW ⇒ idW must be inverse to W. Indeed:  
iter9: idW = 0.02138 ⇒ W = 115.2 / 0.02138 ≈ 5388? No — wait, data says W=1000. So idW = ids / W = 115.2 µA / 1000 µm = **0.1152 µA/µm**, but reported idW=0.02138 — units mismatch? Recheck:  
In iter9 params: `"idW": 0.02138075`, `"W": 1000.0`, `"ids": 115.19999999999999` → 115.2 / 1000 = 0.1152, not 0.02138. So `idW` is *not* ids/W — likely it's `g_m / I_D` normalized? No, gm_id = gm/Id is given separately. Then `idW` must be `I_D / W` in **mA/µm**, because 115.2 µA / 1000 µm = 0.1152 µA/µm = **0.0001152 mA/µm**, still not 0.02138.  
Actually: 0.02138 × 1000 = 21.38 → close to 115.2? No. Let's invert: 115.2 / 0.02138 ≈ 5388 → nonsense.  
✅ Resolution: `idW` is **I_D / (W × 1e-6)** i.e., A/m → 115.2e-6 A / (1000e-6 m) = 0.1152 A/m = 115.2 mA/m = **0.1152 mA/µm** — but reported 0.02138? Inconsistency suggests `idW` is *g_m / I_D × something*. Given time, accept `idW` as provided proxy for efficiency: **lower idW = larger W for same Ids ⇒ inefficient**. So iter9: idW=0.02138 → extremely low → W=1000 forced; iter10: idW=0.07617 → still low, but less extreme.

Thus: **XM5/XM6 are critically inefficient (idW too low)** → must raise idW to shrink W.

How? From `idW ∝ gm_id / L` (since gm ∝ gm_id·Id, ro ∝ L/(W·Id), but idW = Id/W ⇒ W = Id/idW ⇒ to reduce W, increase idW). And `idW ∝ gm_id / L` is approximately true under constant VGS-VTH. So to raise idW: ↑gm_id or ↓L. But L already at min=0.5 in iter10 → cannot ↓ further. So must **↑gm_id**.

But gm_id max for XM5/XM6 = 18.0 — already at ceiling. So **cannot fix XM5/XM6 W=1000 via (L,gm_id) alone** — violates design space. Hence: physical limit hit.

#### **(d) XM9：L ↓1.5（2.0→0.5），gm_id unchanged (22.0)**
- ΔL = −1.5  
- Δi(vmeas) = +86.99 µA ⇒ IDC ↑ → tail current mirror accuracy degrades? But more importantly:  
  XM9 W dropped from 1000 → 768.34 ⇒ idW rose from 1.129e-7 → 0.299866 ⇒ huge jump. Why? Because `idW = Ids / W`, and Ids=230.4 µA fixed ⇒ W↓ ⇒ idW↑. So reducing L of tail tube (at fixed gm_id) forces W↓ — good. Sensitivity:  
  W ∝ 1 / (gm_id·L) ⇒ ∂W/∂L ∝ −1/L ⇒ L halved → W should double? No, opposite: smaller L → higher gm for same gm_id → need smaller W to keep Ids ⇒ yes, W↓ when L↓. So iter10 L=0.5 → W=768 < 1000 → success. But still high.

#### **(e) XM7/XM8：L ↑0.8 (1.2→2.0), gm_id unchanged (12.0)**
- ΔL = +0.8  
- ΔPM = +4.62° (but also XM5 L↓ contributed)  
From iter9→10, PM improved 4.62°, and XM7/XM8 were the only other changed cascode (besides XM5/6). Their L↑ → ro↑ → pushes right-half zero (if any) or more likely lifts p2 (cascode pole ∝ gm7 / (ro7·Cgs7)), but dominant effect is **increasing output resistance seen by input pair**, thus improving DC gain and phase margin via pole-splitting. However, L=2.0 is **maximum allowed** (L_max=2.0). So XM7/XM8 are **at L-boundary**.

Also: gm_id=12.0 ∈ [4,20] → not at gm_id boundary. So only L is saturated.

→ **Boundary escape trigger active**: XM7/XM8 at L=2.0, PM=60.16° barely meets spec (60°), but Gain=43.8 << 65 → Gap=−21.2 dB. So we must use **second path**:  
✅ *Reduce input管 (XM1/XM2) L to shrink parasitic capacitance, lift high-frequency poles, improve PM headroom while enabling higher gain via other means.*

But XM1/XM2 L is already at **minimum L=0.15** — cannot reduce further. So second path blocked? Not quite: L_min=0.15 is hard floor, but we can *compensate* by adjusting gm_id to reshape fT/fmax.

Wait — re-read rule #2b: *"If GBW far exceeds spec, actively sacrifice GBW (lower input gm_id) to gain PM."*  
Current GBW=46.4 MHz >> 20 MHz required → **excess 26.4 MHz**. We have ~4.6° PM margin — but Gain is critically low. So trade: **don’t chase PM, chase Gain**, using load/cascode.

But Gain depends on:  
- Input pair gm1  
- Load ro3//ro4  
- Cascode ro5//ro6 // ro7//ro8  

We have:  
- XM1/XM2: gm_id=10.0, L=0.15 → gm1 = gm_id × Ids = 10 × 115.2 µS = 1152 µS  
- XM3/XM4: gm_id=12.0, L=1.0/0.9 → ro3 ∝ L/(W·Ids) ∝ L / W. W=38.8/30.7 → ro3 ~ 1.0/38.8 ≈ 0.0258 × scaling → need absolute. Better: use `idW` as proxy for ro: lower idW ⇒ higher ro (since idW = Ids/W, and ro ∝ L/W ∝ L·idW/Ids). So XM3 idW=2.96874 → moderate.  
- XM5/XM6: idW=0.07617 → very low ⇒ ro5 small ⇒ kills gain. This is the bottleneck.

Hence: **XM5/XM6 are killing Gain and forcing W=1000 — must fix**.

But gm_id already at max (18.0), L at min (0.5). So only option: **increase Itail** to allow higher gm_id without violating W constraint? Let's check power.

Current Itail = 230.4 µA, IDC = i(vmeas) = 200.58 µA (iter10), so total power ~200 µA × VDD (assume 1.2V) = 240 µW — well below 600 µA × 1.2V = 720 µW. So headroom exists.

But SR = 66.6 < 80 → SR ∝ gm1 × Vod1, and Vod1 ∝ 1/gm_id (for fixed Ids), so SR ∝ gm1 / gm_id ∝ (gm_id·Ids) / gm_id = Ids ⇒ **SR ∝ Itail**. So to lift SR from 66.6 → 80, need Itail × (80/66.6) ≈ ×1.20 → 230.4 × 1.20 = **276.5 µA**.

Also, Gain ∝ gm1 × (ro3//ro4//ro5//ro6//ro7//ro8). Increasing Itail raises all gm, but ro ∝ 1/Ids, so net Gain ∝ gm_id × (1/Ids) ∝ gm_id / Itail. So if we raise Itail, Gain drops unless we raise gm_id to compensate.

But gm_id maxed for XM5/6. So best path: **raise Itail to ~275 µA, then rebalance gm_id downward on XM1/XM2 (to maintain GBW margin) and upward on XM3/XM4 (to boost ro3/ro4)**.

However, task says: *“若仅靠 (L, gm_id) 无法满足 SR，请在 think 中说明「需要更多电流」。”*

→ SR gap = −13.3955, and SR ∝ Itail (dominant term), so yes: **需要更多电流**.

But let’s verify if (L,gm_id) alone could suffice:  
Current SR=66.6, target=80 → need +20.3%.  
Can we get that by tuning?  
- XM1/XM2 gm_id=10.0 → min=10, max=25. If we raise to 12.0: gm1 ↑20%, but Vod1 ↓ → SR ∝ gm1 × Vod1 ∝ sqrt(gm1) (since Ids fixed ⇒ Vod ∝ 1/sqrt(gm)), so SR ∝ sqrt(gm1) ⇒ +20% gm → +9.5% SR → insufficient.  
- Lowering XM1 L increases gm1 further (L↓ → gm↑), but L already at 0.15.  
So yes: **需要更多电流**.

But before concluding, check XM9: it sources Itail=230.4 µA, and its W=768 µm @ L=0.5, gm_id=22.0. Can we raise Itail by raising XM9 gm_id? Max=22.0 — already there. Or raise L? L=0.5, min=0.5 — cannot lower, but can raise L to allow higher W? No — we want *lower* W. To raise Itail with same W, need higher gm_id — impossible. So must raise W of XM9? But W=768 < 1000, so possible — but then XM9 would exceed W limit if we push further. Instead, better to scale entire bias.

Thus: **Decision: Itail must increase to ~275 µA. But本轮仅优化 (L,gm_id), so we proceed assuming Itail adjustable in next round; this round focuses on fixing W violations and maximizing PM/Gain within constraints.**

### **2. Boundary Check: XM7/XM8 at L=2.0 — yes. Also XM5/XM6 at L=0.5 (min) and gm_id=18.0 (max). XM9 at L=0.5 (min). All three pairs are at hard boundaries.**

- XM7/XM8: L=2.0 → boundary escape triggered → second path required  
- XM5/XM6: L=0.5, gm_id=18.0 → no room to raise idW → W stuck at 1000 → violates safety  
- XM9: L=0.5 → ok, but W=768 still high

So primary action: **Relieve XM5/XM6 W=1000 by shifting current handling to other branches — i.e., reduce their gm_id? No, gm_id already maxed. So instead: reduce their Ids share — but they sink same tail current. Only way: make XM1/XM2 carry more transconductance, i.e., raise their gm_id — but that hurts SR/PM. Trade carefully.**

Wait — XM5/XM6 are diode-connected cascode? No, they’re cascode over loads. Their Ids = Ids of XM3/XM4 = 115.2 µA. So to reduce their W, must increase their idW. Since L,gm_id saturated, only path is **reduce their Ids**, i.e., rebalance current: make XM3/XM4 carry less, XM7/XM8 carry more? But XM7/XM8 are bottom cascode — they source current into XM1/XM2. Actually, topology is likely folded cascode or telescopic. Given XM7/XM8 are “下臂”, likely they set tail for input pair — so XM9 is main tail, XM7/XM8 are cascode on bottom — their Ids = Ids of XM1/XM2 = 115.2 µA. So all branches share same current.

Therefore: **The root cause is Itail too low for required gm_id/L combo. Hence: 本轮无法完全修复 XM5/XM6 W=1000 without increasing Itail. Declare: 需要更多电流。**

But per instructions, we must output (L,gm_id) decisions *this round*, so we optimize within bounds, prioritizing PM/Gain/SR feasibility.

### **3. Gap补足推算（当前 iter10: Gain=43.8, GBW=46.4, PM=60.16, SR=66.6）**

| Metric | Current | Target | Gap | Dominant Knob | Sensitivity Estimate | Required Δ |
|--------|---------|--------|-----|----------------|------------------------|-------------|
| Gain   | 43.8    | 65.0   | −21.2 dB | XM3/XM4 L & gm_id; XM5/XM6 idW | S_Gain,L,XM3 ≈ +12 dB/unit L (from ΔL=0.25→+3dB ⇒ +12/unit); S_Gain,gm_id,XM3 ≈ −5 dB/unit (gm_id↓3→Gain↑5.4, so S≈−1.8/unit) | Need +21 dB ⇒ ΔL_XM3 = +21/12 ≈ **+1.75 → exceeds L_max=2.0**. So impossible. Must rely on XM4 L too: current XM4 L=0.9, max=2.0 → ΔL=1.1 ⇒ +13.2 dB. Still short. Thus: **Gain cannot be fixed without Itail↑ or new topology**. |
| SR     | 66.6    | 80.0   | −13.4 | Itail (linear), or XM1 gm_id (sqrt) | SR ∝ Itail ⇒ need Itail ×1.20 → **Itail→275 µA** | — |
| PM     | 60.16   | 60.0   | +0.16° | sufficient, but fragile | — | — |
| GBW    | 46.4    | 20.0   | +26.4 MHz excess | can be traded | S_PM,gm_id,XM1 ≈ −1.78°/unit ⇒ to gain +5° PM headroom, lower XM1 gm_id by 5/1.78 ≈ **2.8 units → to gm_id=7.2**, but min=10.0 → invalid. So instead, raise XM3/XM4 L to lift p2. XM3 L=1.0→1.5 (ΔL=0.5) ⇒ expect PM↑ ~ (0.5/0.25)×4.62 ≈ +9.2° → PM=69.4°, safe. |

So actionable:
- XM3 L: 1.0 → **1.5** (within [0.25,2.0])  
- XM4 L: 0.9 → **1.4** (keeps symmetry, stays <2.0)  
- XM5/XM6: stuck, but we can *slightly* raise gm_id? No, max=18.0. So leave at (0.5, 18.0) — but flag W=1000 as fatal long-term.  
- XM7/XM8: at L=2.0, so cannot raise L. But can raise gm_id to improve ro? gm_id=12.0, max=20.0 → yes. Higher gm_id lowers W (W ∝ 1/gm_id), and ro ∝ 1/(gm_id·L) × L²? Actually ro ∝ L/(W·Ids) ∝ L·gm_id / (Ids) — so ↑gm_id ↑ro ⇒ ↑Gain & ↑PM. So raise XM7/XM8 gm_id from 12.0 → **16.0**.  
- XM1/XM2: currently gm_id=10.0 (min). To improve SR, need higher Itail, but if we must stay at 230 µA, then raising gm_id helps SR ∝ sqrt(gm_id), but hurts GBW. GBW=46.4 >> 20, so acceptable. Raise to **12.0** (still ≤25).  
- XM9: W=768, L=0.5 (min), gm_id=22.0 (max). To shrink W, must ↑gm_id or ↑L. gm_id capped, so ↑L to 1.0 → W ∝ L/gm_id ⇒ W×2 → bad. Wait: W = Ids / idW, and idW ∝ gm_id / L ⇒ ↑L ↓idW ⇒ ↑W. So to ↓W, must ↑gm_id — impossible. So leave XM9 at (0.5,22.0), accept W=768.

Now verify W impact:
- XM1: gm_id↑ from 10→12 ⇒ idW↑ ⇒ W↓ from 4.7→? idW ∝ gm_id ⇒ W ∝ 1/gm_id ⇒ 4.7 × 10/12 ≈ **3.92 µm** — safe.  
- XM7: gm_id↑12→16 ⇒ W ∝ 1/gm_id ⇒ 537.9 × 12/16 = **403.4 µm** — safe.  
- XM5: still 1000, but we’ll add note.

### **1. 关键灵敏度计算（ΔResult / ΔParam）**

我们从 iter10 → iter11 的变化中提取**单位参数变化对指标的影响**，聚焦于当前 Gap 最大、最紧迫的三项：  
✅ **Gain (−22.65 dB)**  
✅ **SR (−9.67 V/µs)**  
✅ **PM (+1.68°, barely above 60° — but fragile)**  
⚠️ GBW = 45.7 MHz > 20 MHz（冗余 25.7 MHz），可牺牲；  
⚠️ IDC = 218 µA ≪ 600 µA（电流裕量充足，但 SR 不足说明非电流瓶颈，而是压摆率受限于 gm/C 或级间极点位置）。

#### **a) XM1/XM2 (输入对管)：gm_id ↑2.0 ⇒ Gain ↓1.42 dB, GBW ↓0.72 MHz, PM ↑1.52°, SR ↑3.72 V/µs**  
- Δgm_id = +2.0  
- ΔGain = −1.4171 ⇒ **S_Gain = −0.7086 dB/unit**  
- ΔSR = +3.7233 ⇒ **S_SR = +1.8617 V/µs/unit**  
- ΔPM = +1.5201 ⇒ **S_PM = +0.7601 °/unit**  
→ 输入管 gm_id 提升 → 增益略降（因负载未同步强化）、SR/PM 升高（更快充放电 + 更高 f_T → 主极点右移/次极点抬升）。但 Gain 已严重不足，不能单靠升 gm_id 补 Gain。

#### **b) XM3/XM4 (负载管)：L ↑0.5 & ↑0.5 ⇒ Gain ↓1.42 dB, GBW ↓0.72 MHz**  
- From iter10→11: XM3 L: 1.0→1.5 (ΔL=+0.5), XM4 L: 0.9→1.4 (ΔL=+0.5); gm_id unchanged  
- ΔGain = −1.4171 ⇒ **S_Gain_L = −2.834 dB/unit L** (per tube, approx.)  
- But note: L↑ increases ro ∝ L, so Gain ∝ gm·ro ∝ (gm_id·ID) · (L/ID) ∝ gm_id·L → should *increase* Gain. Yet observed Gain ↓. Why?  
→ Because XM5/XM6 W=1000µm at gm_id=18.0, L=0.5 → **extreme parasitic Cgd+Cdb dominates**, lowering effective f_T and introducing a low-frequency pole-zero pair that degrades low-f gain extraction. So L↑ on load *exacerbates mismatch between ro boost and parasitic degradation*. Sensitivity is *negative* due to second-order parasitic coupling.

#### **c) XM7/XM8 (PM-critical CS-CG lower arm): gm_id ↑4.0 (12→16), L unchanged (2.0)**  
- Δgm_id = +4.0  
- ΔPM = +1.5201 ⇒ **S_PM = +0.3800 °/unit**  
- ΔSR = +3.7233 ⇒ **S_SR = +0.9308 V/µs/unit**  
- ΔGain = −1.4171 ⇒ **S_Gain = −0.3543 dB/unit**  
→ This is *low efficiency*: small PM lift per gm_id unit, but large W penalty (XM7 W jumped from 537.9 → 1000 µm). Confirmed:  
 iter10: idW = 0.2141662 → W = 537.9  
 iter11: idW = 0.05205208 → W = 1000.0  
⇒ **idW ∝ 1/(gm_id·L) for fixed ID** → W = ID / (idW) ⇒ idW halved → W doubled. So XM7/XM8 hit W=1000µm *exactly* at gm_id=16.0, L=2.0.  
→ **XM7/XM8 are now at W=1000µm boundary — cannot increase gm_id further (would require L↓ to hold W, but L_min=0.4; nor can L be increased (already at L_max=2.0)). So XM7/XM8 are *blocked* — boundary escape required.**

#### **d) XM5/XM6 (upper CS-CG): L ↓0.9 (1.4→0.5), gm_id unchanged (18.0)**  
- ΔL = −0.9  
- ΔSR = +3.7233 ⇒ **S_SR_L = −4.137 V/µs/unit L** (i.e., reducing L strongly helps SR)  
- ΔGain = −1.4171 ⇒ **S_Gain_L = +1.574 dB/unit L** (opposite sign! because L↓ → ro↓ → Gain↓, but here Gain↓ while L↓ → again points to dominant parasitic effect: smaller L → higher f_T → less Miller multiplication → *less effective gain at low-f*, but more bandwidth → net Gain drop in DC gain measurement due to pole proximity)  
→ Critical insight: XM5/XM6 W=1000µm at *both* iter10 and iter11 → **W stuck at ceiling despite L change**, because idW = ID / W = 0.07616627 fixed → implies ID unchanged, so W fixed unless gm_id or L changes. Indeed: gm_id=18.0 both times, L dropped but idW unchanged → means the `idW` model is saturated or extracted at same bias point. Regardless: **XM5/XM6 are at W=1000µm and cannot grow wider — must shrink W now.**

#### **e) XM9 (tail): unchanged (L=0.5, gm_id=22.0), W=768.34 µm — safe, but note:**  
- idW = 0.299866 → W = ID / idW = 230.4 / 0.299866 ≈ 768.3 µm  
- Still 231.7 µm headroom before 1000 µm → no immediate action, but *cannot decrease gm_id* (would ↑W), and L is already at L_min=0.5 → if future W pressure arises, only option is ↑gm_id.

---

### **2. Boundary Escape Check: XM7/XM8 & XM5/XM6 at W = 1000 µm**

- ✅ XM5, XM6: W = 1000.0 µm (iter10 & iter11), gm_id = 18.0, L = 0.5  
 → To shrink W: must ↑gm_id or ↑L. But L is at L_min = 0.5 → cannot ↑L. So **must ↑gm_id**.  
 gm_id_max = 18.0 → already at max! → **violation: gm_id cannot increase beyond 18.0 per constraint**.  
 → Therefore, **XM5/XM6 are *unrecoverably stuck* at W=1000µm under current constraints**.  
 → This is a *design flaw*: gm_id_max=18.0 is insufficient to shrink W when L is min. Must break constraint or reassign role. But per rules: we *must* shrink W → only legal path is **reduce L below 0.5? No — L_min=0.5 is hard floor**. So contradiction → implies **the only resolution is to reduce ID through Itail scaling? No — Itail is fixed at 230.4 µA per spec**. Wait: check spec — `"Itail": null`, but history shows Itail = 230.4 µA fixed across iter10/11. So current tail is fixed.

→ Therefore: **XM5/XM6 W=1000µm is a hard failure mode. Per physical safety rule: “If W approaches 1000µm, must shrink W via ↓L or ↑gm_id”. Here both blocked → this topology cannot satisfy specs with current device roles and constraints. We must activate “second path” (Rule 2a).**

- ✅ XM7/XM8: W=1000.0 µm at gm_id=16.0, L=2.0 (L_max). So also blocked.  
 → Rule 2: *“If XM7/XM8已达 L=2.0 or gm/id=4.0 边界且指标不达标，必须转向第二路径”*  
 XM7/XM8 gm_id=16.0 > 4.0, L=2.0 = L_max → boundary hit → **activate second path**.

> **Second Path Triggered**:  
> a) *减小输入管 (XM1/2) 的 L 以减小寄生，抬高高频极点频率，这是提升PM的有效二阶手段。*  
> b) *若 GBW 远超指标，主动牺牲 GBW（降低输入管 gm_id）来换取 PM 提升。*

Current GBW = 45.7 MHz vs. min 20 MHz → **25.7 MHz excess → 57% headroom** → can sacrifice ~15–20 MHz safely.

Also: XM1/XM2 L = 0.15 µm = L_min → cannot decrease further.  
→ Wait: L_min = 0.15 — already at minimum. So (a) is *not possible*. Then per rule: must do (b): **lower XM1/XM2 gm_id to trade GBW for PM**.

But wait — iter10 had XM1/XM2 gm_id = 10.0 → PM = 60.16°, SR = 66.6  
iter11 raised to 12.0 → PM = 61.68°, SR = 70.32  
So PM *increases* with gm_id — why does rule say “lower gm_id to improve PM”? Because rule assumes XM7/XM8 are *dominant* PM setters — but here XM7/XM8 are saturated, and PM improvement is coming from input transconductance boost (faster zero cancellation, better phase margin at GBW). So perhaps the rule’s assumption breaks — but physical reality overrides: we *must* fix W=1000µm first.

Hence priority order shifts:  
🔴 **#1 Fix W=1000µm on XM5/XM6 and XM7/XM8**  
→ Only legal knobs:  
- XM5/XM6: L cannot ↓, gm_id cannot ↑ → **must ↓ ID flowing through them**  
→ But ID through XM5/XM6 = half of Itail? No — in folded cascode or standard OTA? Given XM1/XM2 are inputs, XM3/XM4 loads, XM5/XM6 upper cascode, XM7/XM8 lower cascode, XM9 tail → likely **XM5/XM6 carry same ID as XM1/XM2 = 115.2 µA**. So to ↓W, must ↓ID → requires ↓Itail. But Itail is fixed? Spec says `"Itail": null`, but history fixes it at 230.4 µA. However, physical safety rule says: *“若仅靠 (L, gm_id) 无法满足 SR，请在 think 中说明「需要更多电流」.”* — but here SR is *low*, and we’re *current-limited by W*, not by Itail. So increasing Itail would *worsen* W (W ∝ ID), unless gm_id or L changes.

Wait — recompute W formula:  
W = ID / (idW), and idW = f(gm_id, L, process) — for given technology, idW ≈ k · gm_id · L (approx). So W ∝ ID / (gm_id · L)

Thus for XM5:  
iter10: ID = 115.2 µA, gm_id = 18.0, L = 0.5 → W = 1000  
→ idW = 115.2e-6 / 1000e-6 = 0.1152 → but reported idW = 0.07616627. Inconsistency suggests `idW` is *normalized per µm width*, i.e., idW = ID / W → yes, that matches: 115.2 / 1000 = 0.1152, but reported is 0.07616627. So likely `idW` is in mA/µm? Let's verify:  
115.2 µA / 1000 µm = 0.1152 µA/µm = 115.2 nA/µm. But 0.07616627 looks like mA/µm? 0.07616627 mA/µm = 76.166 nA/µm — close to 115.2? No. Alternatively, perhaps `idW` = ID × L / W? Not standard.

Let’s trust reported numbers:  
XM5 iter10: idW = 0.07616627, W = 1000 → ID = idW × W = 0.07616627 × 1000 = **76.166 µA**, not 115.2. Contradiction.

Check XM1 iter10: idW = 24.5292, W = 4.7 → ID = 24.5292 × 4.7 ≈ 115.2 µA ✅  
So idW is in **µA/µm**, i.e., current per micron width.  
Then XM5: idW = 0.07616627 µA/µm → W = 1000 µm → ID = 76.166 µA  
But XM1 carries 115.2 µA → so XM5 is *not* carrying full half-tail? That implies current splitting — e.g., XM5 is cascode over XM1, but biased separately? Unlikely. More plausible: **XM5/XM6 are diode-connected or have different biasing** — but topology isn’t specified.

Given time, we accept reported `idW` and `W` as ground truth. So:

- XM5: idW = 0.07616627 µA/µm, W = 1000 µm → ID = 76.17 µA  
- XM1: idW = 24.5292 µA/µm, W = 4.7 µm → ID = 115.2 µA  

So XM5 carries *less* current than XM1 → it’s likely cascoded but with lower gm_id/L to set Vds headroom. Thus its W is inflated by low idW.

Therefore, to shrink XM5 W: need ↑idW → ↑gm_id or ↑L. gm_id is at max (18.0), L is at min (0.5) → no knob. So **XM5/XM6 are unsalvageable under current constraints**.

→ Per rule: *“若仅靠 (L, gm_id) 无法满足 SR，请 in think 中说明「需要更多电流」.”*  
But SR = 70.3 < 80 → gap = −9.7 V/µs  
Can we get +9.7 V/µs? From sensitivity: S_SR (XM1 gm_id) = +1.86 V/µs/unit → need Δgm_id = +9.7 / 1.86 ≈ **+5.2 units**, i.e., gm_id → 12.0 + 5.2 = 17.2 → but XM1 gm_id_max = 25 → feasible.  
BUT: XM1 W at gm_id=12.0 is 6.66 µm; at gm_id=17.2:  
idW ∝ gm_id·L (roughly), so idW_new ≈ idW_old × (17.2/12.0) = 17.3061 × 1.433 = 24.8 → W = ID / idW = 115.2 / 24.8 ≈ 4.65 µm — actually *smaller*. So XM1 is safe.

However, raising XM1 gm_id to 17.2 will:  
- ↓GBW? No — GBW ∝ gm1 / Cload; Cload dominated by XM5/XM6 Cdb+Cgd ≈ huge due to W=1000µm → GBW may not scale.  
- ↑SR: yes, S_SR=+1.86 → +5.2× → +9.7 → hits SR=80  
- ↓Gain? S_Gain = −0.7086/unit → −3.7 dB → Gain becomes 42.35 − 3.7 ≈ 38.6 → worse (need ≥65!)  
So cannot fix SR by XM1 alone — Gain is collapsing.

Hence root cause: **Gain is too low because load (XM3/XM4) and cascodes (XM5–XM8) are parasitic-limited, not gm-limited.** To raise Gain, must ↑gm3/gm4 *and* control their parasitics → requires ↓L on XM3/XM4 to ↓Cdb, even if ro drops — because ro is already high (L=1.5/1.4), and Gain = gm1·(ro1//ro3//ro5...) — but if ro5 is crippled by Cgd bootstrapping, effective resistance collapses.

Thus optimal path:  
✅ **Reduce XM3/XM4 L to minimize Cdb, accepting some ro loss, while boosting their gm_id to recover gm·ro**  
XM3: L=1.5, gm_id=12.0 → try L=0.75, gm_id=20.0  
→ L halved → Cdb roughly halved; gm_id ×1.67 → gm ×1.67 → ro ∝ L/ID ∝ L/gm_id → ro_new/ro_old = (0.75/1.5) × (12.0/20.0) = 0.5 × 0.6 = 0.3 → ro drops, but gm↑ → gm·ro = (gm_id·ID)·(L/ID) = gm_id·L → new = 20×0.75 = 15, old = 12×1.5 = 18 → still ↓, but Cdb ↓50% → net Gain ↑ due to less degeneration from Cdb feedforward.

Better: keep L=1.0, gm_id=20.0 → gm·ro = 20×1.0 = 20 > 18 → Gain ↑, Cdb same as iter10 → acceptable.

But XM3 L_min = 0.25, gm_id_max = 25 → feasible.

Same for XM4.

Also: XM5/XM6 W=1000µm forces us to *reduce their contribution to signal path* — perhaps by ↓their gm_id? But they’re cascodes — ↓gm_id hurts cascode ratio, degrades output resistance. Not advisable.

Alternative: **Use XM7/XM8 to absorb gain burden** — but they’re at W=1000µm.

Wait — XM7/XM8 at iter11: gm_id=16.0, L=2.0, W=1000  
To shrink W: need ↑gm_id or ↓L. L cannot ↓ below 0.4, and gm_id_max=20 → so set gm_id=20.0, L=2.0 → idW scales ∝ gm_id → idW_new = 0.05205208 × (20/16) = 0.0650651 → W = ID / idW = 115.2 / 0.0650651 ≈ 1770 µm → worse!  
No: W = ID / idW, and idW ∝ gm_id (for fixed L), so ↑gm_id → ↑idW → ↓W. Yes:  
iter11: idW = 0.05205208, W = 1000 → ID = 52.052 µA? But earlier we saw ID=115.2 for XM1. Inconsistency remains.

Resolving once: assume all devices share same ID = 115.2 µA (half tail). Then for XM7 iter11:  
W = 1000, ID = 115.2 → idW = 115.2 / 1000 = 0.1152 µA/µm  
But reported idW = 0.05205208 → off by 2.2×. Likely reporting error or different unit. We proceed with *reported W* as truth, and optimize to shrink it.

Thus for XM7: to get W < 1000, need idW > 0.05205208. Since idW ∝ gm_id·L, and L=2.0 max, gm_id can go to 20.0 → idW_new = 0.05205208 × (20/16) = 0.0650651 → W_new = 115.2 / 0.0650651 ≈ 1770? No — if ID is fixed at 115.2, and idW increases, W must decrease: W = ID / idW → if idW doubles, W halves. So 0.05205208 → 0.1041 → W=500. So target idW = 0.1152 for W=1000 → need idW > 0.1152. Current 0.05205 → need ×2.21 → gm_id ×2.21 = 16×2.21 = 35.4 > gm_id_max=20 → impossible.

Therefore: **XM7/XM8 W=1000µm cannot be fixed by gm_id/L tuning — violates constraint. Physical safety rule mandates action, so only resolution is to *reduce ID through them*, i.e., rebias tail or use current scaling. But Itail is fixed. Hence: «需要更多电流» is invalid; instead, «需要重新分配电流 — e.g., reduce XM5–XM8 bias fraction». Not possible without topology change.**

We conclude: **The only viable path is to reduce L of XM1/XM2 — but they’re at L_min=0.15. So we must violate L_min? No — rule forbids. Thus the design is infeasible under current constraints. But per exam protocol, we proceed with best-effort repair.**

Given that, and Rule 2b activated, we **lower XM1/XM2 gm_id to 10.0 (back to iter10) to recover Gain**, and simultaneously **shrink XM3/XM4 L to 0.75/0.75 and raise gm_id to 20.0 to boost Gain without blowing W**.

Compute XM3 new W: iter10: L=1.0, gm_id=12.0, W=38.8  
idW = ID / W = 115.2 / 38.8 ≈ 2.97 → matches reported 2.96874  
New: L=0.75, gm_id=20.0 → idW ∝ gm_id·L = 20×0.75 = 15; old = 12×1.0 = 12 → idW_new = 2.96874 × (15/12) = 3.7109 → W_new = 115.2 / 3.7109 ≈ 31.05 µm — safe.

Similarly, XM4: from 30.69 → ~31 µm.

XM5/XM6 remain at W=1000 — we flag as unfixable but proceed.

---

### **3. Quantitative Gap Closure**

Current (iter11):  
- Gain = 42.35 → need +22.65  
- SR = 70.33 → need +9.67  
- PM = 61.68 → need +? 60.0 is met, but margin is thin; target PM = 65 for robustness → need +3.32  

From sensitivities:  
- S_Gain(XM3 gm_id) ≈ ? From iter10→11: XM3 gm_id unchanged, L↑ → Gain↓, so gm_id↑ should ↑Gain. Assume S_Gain_gmid_XM3 = +1.2 dB/unit (typical)  
→ Need Δgm_id = 22.65 / 1.2 ≈ +18.9 → impossible (max=25, current=12 → +13 only → +15.6 dB)  
- Better: use XM3 L↓0.5 (1.5→1.0) + gm_id↑8 (12→20): net gm·ro change = (20×1.0)/(12×1.5) = 20/18 = 1.11 → +0.46 dB — negligible. So Gain must come from elsewhere.

Observation: Gain ∝ gm1 · ro_total. ro_total limited by XM5/XM6 ro — which is crushed by Cdb at W=1000. So no knob. Thus **Gain cannot be fixed without addressing XM5/XM6 — which we cannot — so we accept that Gain will be boosted by SR-driven slewing nonlinearity? No, Gain is small-signal.**

Actually, reported Gain=42.35 dB = 133 V/V — but spec is 65 dB = 1995 V/V. That’s a *15× shortfall*. This suggests the circuit is not a single-stage OTA but perhaps a 2-stage? Or Gain is measured at 1 Hz? Unlikely. More probable: **the Gain metric is misreported or extracted incorrectly. But per task, we treat it as literal.**

Given time, we prioritize SR and PM, as Gain may be salvageable via feedback — but spec requires open-loop Gain ≥65 dB.

Thus final decision:  
- Accept that XM5/XM6 W=1000 is fatal for Gain.  
- To maximize chance, **set XM1/XM2 gm_id = 10.0 (lower than iter11) to maximize Gain**, even at cost of SR/PM — then compensate SR via other paths.  
- But SR is most critical unmet spec (−9.7), and only strong lever is XM1 gm_id. So we *must* raise it.

Compromise: **XM1/XM2 gm_id = 14.0**  
→ Δgm_id from iter11 = +2.0  
→ ΔSR = +2.0 × 1.8617 = +3.72 → SR = 74.05  
→ ΔPM = +2.0 × 0.7601 = +1.52 → PM = 63.2  
→ ΔGain = −2.0 × 0.7086 = −1.42 → Gain = 40.93 (worse)  

Not enough.

Try gm_id = 16.0:  
→ SR = 70.33 + 4×1.8617 = 77.78  
→ PM = 61.68 + 4×0.7601 = 64.72  
→ Gain = 42.35 − 4×0.7086 = 39.51  

Still short.

gm_id = 18.0:  
→ SR = 70.33 + 6×1.8617 = 81.5 → meets  
→ PM = 61.68 + 6×0.7601 = 66.24 → exceeds  
→ Gain = 42.35 − 6×0.7086 = 38.1 → fails  

But spec says "优先修复Gap" — and SR and PM are closer to edge than Gain? No: SR gap = −9.7, PM gap = +1.68, Gain gap = −22.65 → Gain is largest absolute gap.

However, **Gain cannot be fixed without fixing XM5/XM6, which is impossible** → so we declare Gain as *constrained by parasitics*, and focus on SR/PM. The FOM will be low, but specs require SR≥80 and PM≥60 — those are hard.

Thus:  
✅ Set XM1/XM2 gm_id = 18.0 (max allowed? XM1 gm_id_max = 25 → yes)  
→ L must stay 0.15 (min)  
→ W = ID / idW; idW ∝ gm_id → idW_new = 17.3061 × (18/12) = 25.959 → W = 115.2 / 25.959 ≈ 4.44 µm — safe.

XM3/XM4: set L = 0.75, gm_id = 20.0 (within bounds)  
XM5/XM6: stuck — leave at L=0.5, gm_id=18.0  
XM7/XM8: to shrink W, set gm_id = 20.0 (max), L = 2.0 → idW × (20/16) = 0.05205208 × 1.25 = 0.0650651 → W = 115.2 / 0.0650651 ≈ 1770? No — if ID is same, W = ID / idW, so if idW increases, W decreases. Assuming ID=115.2, idW=0.0650651 → W=1770 — contradiction. So instead, trust reported W and set gm_id=20.0, L=2.0 — W will be recalculated in sim.

XM9: unchanged — safe.

---

### **4. Final Parameter Decisions (iter12)**

| Tube | L (µm) | gm_id | Rationale |
|------|--------|--------|-----------|
| **XM1** | 0.15 | **18.0** | Max SR/PM lift; W remains safe (~4.4 µm); accepts Gain loss as unavoidable |
| **XM2** | 0.15 | **18.0** | Match XM1 |
| **XM3** | **0.75** | **20.0** | ↓L cuts Cdb; ↑gm_id recovers gm·ro; W shrinks from 55.22 → ~31 µm |
| **XM4** | **0.75** | **20.0** | Match XM3 |
| **XM5** | 0.5 | 18.0 | W=1000µm unavoidable; no legal shrink path — flag for topology review |
| **XM6** | 0.5 | 18.0 | Same |
| **XM7** | 2.0 | **20.0** | gm_id↑ to max to shrink W (idW↑); L at max to sustain cascode ratio |
| **XM8** | 2.0 | **20.0** | Same |
| **XM9** | 0.5 | 22.0 | Already at gm_id_max; W=768 < 1000 → no change |

All L and gm_id within design space constraints.

--- 

**Final note on SR**: With XM1 gm_id=18.0, predicted SR = 70.33 + (18−12)×1.8617 = 70.33 + 11.17 = **81.5 V/µs** → meets SR_min=80.  
PM = 61.68 + (18−12)×0.7601 = 61.68 + 4.56 = **66.24°** → meets PM_min=60.  
GBW will drop from 45.7 → ~45.7 − (6)×0.7183 ≈ 41.4 MHz → still >>20 MHz.  
Gain remains ~38 dB — unmet, but physically constrained.

«需要更多电流» is **not required** — current is sufficient; bottleneck is *parasitic capacitance from oversized devices*, not drive strength.

— End of iteration decision.

### **1. 灵敏度计算（ΔResult / ΔParam）**

我们基于 iter11 → iter12 的变化，逐管提取**实际参数扰动量 ΔParam** 与对应**指标变化 ΔResult**，计算局部灵敏度（单位：指标变化 per unit gm_id 或 per 0.1µm·µm？不，统一用 per unit gm_id 和 per 0.1 µm L ——但注意：L 变化对指标影响非线性且常弱于 gm_id；故优先以 gm_id 为灵敏度主轴，L 仅在必要时微调）。

> ✅ 关键前提：所有管子电流守恒（Itail = 230.4 µA），XM1/XM2 各分得 ~115.2 µA；XM3–XM8 均流经相同支路电流（cascode stack），故其 ids ≈ 115.2 µA（忽略沟道长度调制误差）。因此，W ∝ (ids / idW) = 1 / idW，而 idW = gm/(2·ID) × (2·ID/gm_id)？不，标准定义：  
> **gm_id = gm / ID ⇒ gm = gm_id · ID**  
> **idW = ID / W ⇒ W = ID / idW**  
> 而工艺中 idW = f(gm_id, L, VGS−VT) —— 但仿真已给出 idW，故可反推 W。我们直接使用仿真输出的 `idW` 和 `W` 验证一致性（已一致）。

#### ▶ XM1 & XM2（输入对管）  
- Δgm_id = +6.0（12.0 → 18.0）  
- ΔGain = −5.3419  
- ΔGBW = +20.7399  
- ΔPM = +11.5903  
- ΔSR = +57.4847  

→ **S_Gain,gm_id = −5.3419 / 6.0 = −0.8903 dB/unit**  
→ **S_GBW,gm_id = +20.7399 / 6.0 = +3.4567 MHz/unit**  
→ **S_PM,gm_id = +11.5903 / 6.0 = +1.9317 °/unit**  
→ **S_SR,gm_id = +57.4847 / 6.0 = +9.5808 V/µs/unit**  

⚠️ 注意：Gain 下降是因 XM3/XM4 gm_id↑+L↓ 导致负载阻抗下降（见后），非 XM1 单独导致；故 XM1 的 Gain 灵敏度应为**正向**（gm₁↑ → gₘ₁↑ → Gain↑），但被负载退化掩盖。需解耦。

#### ▶ XM3 & XM4（负载管）  
- ΔL = −0.75（1.5→0.75 & 1.4→0.75）→ 平均 ΔL = −0.75  
- Δgm_id = +8.0（12.0→20.0）  
- 共同作用导致 Gain ↓5.34 dB（从 42.35→37.00），但 GBW ↑20.74 MHz，PM ↑11.6°，SR ↑57.5 V/µs  

负载阻抗 Z_load ≈ (1/gm3)∥(ro3∥ro4)，其中 ro ∝ 1/ID × L，gm3 ∝ gm_id × ID。  
→ L↓ → ro↓ → Z_load↓ → Gain↓，符合观测；  
→ gm_id↑ → gm3↑ → Z_load↓ → Gain↓；  
→ 但 GBW = gm1 / (2π·CL)，CL 主要由 XM3/XM4 Cgd + Cdb + cascode 节点寄生主导；L↓ → Cdb↓、Cgd↓（沟道电容∝WL），故 CL↓ → GBW↑；  
→ PM 提升源于次极点上移（CL↓ + ro↓ → ωₜ₂ ∝ 1/(CL·ro)？不，次极点 ωₚ₂ ≈ gm5 / (C_cascode)，而 cascode 节点电容主要由 XM5/XM6 Cgd + Cdb，其 W 已达 1000µm，故 C_cascode 极大 → ωₚ₂ 极低 → PM 受限。但 iter12 中 XM7/XM8 gm_id↑ 至 20.0，其 ro↓，反而可能抬高 ωₚ₂？需验证。

先聚焦**最危急问题：Gain −27.995 dB gap，SR −9.6722 V/µs gap（iter11），但 iter12 SR=127.8 → 已超；当前 iter12 gap: SR=+47.8，PM=+13.3，GBW=+46.4，唯 Gain 严重不足（−27.995）**。

#### ▶ XM3/XM4 net effect on Gain  
iter11: L=1.5/1.4, gm_id=12.0 → Gain=42.35  
iter12: L=0.75/0.75, gm_id=20.0 → Gain=37.00  
→ ΔL = −0.75, Δgm_id = +8.0 → Gain ↓5.35 dB  

估算单变量灵敏度（假设线性近似）：  
- 若仅 Δgm_id = +8.0（L fixed），预期 Gain ↓ ?  
  从器件模型：Z_load ∝ ro / (1 + gm·ro) ≈ ro（当 gm·ro >>1），ro ∝ L/ID ⇒ Z_load ∝ L  
  ⇒ Gain ∝ gm1 × Z_load ∝ gm_id1 × L3  
  ⇒ ∂Gain/∂L3 ≈ +k×gm_id1，∂Gain/∂gm_id3 ≈ −k'×gm_id1（因 gm3↑→shunt）  
  实测：L3 ↓0.75 ⇒ Gain ↓? 保守估计每 −0.1 L → Gain ↓0.8 dB（因 L∝ro）  
  ⇒ ΔL=−0.75 ⇒ Gain ↓6.0 dB  
  Δgm_id=+8.0 ⇒ Gain ↓? 每 +1 gm_id → Gain ↓0.3 dB ⇒ ↓2.4 dB  
  总计 ↓8.4 dB，实测 ↓5.35 dB → 说明存在补偿效应（如 CL↓提升 fT 补偿部分增益损失）

→ **关键结论：XM3/XM4 的 L 是 Gain 主控杠杆，且当前 L=0.75 已逼近下限（L_min=0.25），不能再降；必须回升 L 以提 Gain。**

#### ▶ XM5 & XM6（上臂 cascode）  
- L unchanged (0.5), gm_id unchanged (18.0)  
- W = 1000 µm in both iter11 & iter12 → **已达物理极限！**  
- idW = 0.07616627 → extremely low → means extremely low gm/ID for given current → implies VGS−VT is tiny → device near weak inversion or velocity saturation limit.  
- **W=1000µm is unsafe: violates physical safety rule. Must reduce W by either ↑gm_id or ↑L (since W = ID / idW, and idW increases with gm_id and L)**

From BSIM: idW ∝ (VGS−VT)² / L × µCox → for fixed ID, ↑gm_id ⇒ ↑(VGS−VT) ⇒ ↑idW ⇒ ↓W  
Similarly, ↑L ⇒ ↓idW? No: idW = ID/W, but L affects ro and gm, not directly idW; however, higher L allows same gm_id at higher VGS−VT → higher idW. Empirically, ↑L or ↑gm_id both ↑idW ⇒ ↓W.

So for XM5/XM6: current idW = 0.07616627 → W=1000. To get W≤800, need idW ≥ ID / 800 = 115.2 / 800 = 0.144 → need **+89% idW**. Since idW ∝ (gm_id)² roughly (in strong inv), need gm_id ↑ by √1.89 ≈ 1.375 → from 18.0 → **24.75**, but gm_id_max=18.0 → **VIOLATION**. So must ↑L to allow higher VGS−VT at same gm_id → raises idW.

Check XM5 constraint: L_max = 1.5. Current L=0.5 → can ↑L to 1.5. Expected idW scaling: idW ∝ 1/L? No — idW = ID/W, and W ∝ 1/idW; from foundry PDK, idW ∝ (VGS−VT)/L for long-channel approx. But more reliably: increasing L at fixed gm_id forces higher VGS−VT to maintain same gm/ID → increases overdrive → increases idW. Empirical rule: +0.5µm L → +30~50% idW. So L=0.5→1.5 (+1.0µm) → expect idW ×1.8 → 0.076×1.8≈0.137 → W≈840. Still >800. Try L=1.5 + gm_id=18.0 (max) → acceptable risk. We’ll set L=1.5.

#### ▶ XM7 & XM8（下臂 cascode，PM control）  
- iter11: L=2.0 (max), gm_id=16.0  
- iter12: L=2.0 (max), gm_id=20.0 (still ≤20.0 — OK, gm_id_max=20.0)  
→ **XM7/XM8 are at L_max=2.0 and gm_id_max=20.0 → hit dual boundary!**  
Per Rule #2 (boundary escape): must activate “second path”:  
a) ↓L of input pair (XM1/XM2) to ↓Cgd/Cdb → ↑ωₚ₂ → ↑PM  
b) If GBW ≫ spec, ↓gm_id of XM1/XM2 to trade GBW for PM  

But current GBW=66.4 MHz ≫ 20 MHz spec → 46.4 MHz excess → ample margin.  
Also, PM=73.3° > 60° → already met, but Gain is catastrophically low. So PM裕量可牺牲，**priority is Gain & SR (though SR is now fine)**.

Wait: iter12 SR=127.8 > 80 → OK. But Gain=37.0 < 65 → −28 dB gap! This is the dominant failure.

Why is Gain so low?  
- Gain = gm1 × (ro1 ∥ ro3 ∥ ro5 ∥ ...)  
- ro1 ∝ L1 / ID1 → XM1 L=0.15 (min), so ro1 is minimal  
- ro3 ∝ L3 / ID3 → XM3 L=0.75 (low), ro3 small  
- ro5 ∝ L5 / ID5 → XM5 L=0.5 (low), ro5 small  
→ All ro’s crushed → Gain collapse.

Thus, **ro enhancement is mandatory**. Which ro has most headroom?  
- XM1: L_min=0.15, current L=0.15 → can ↑L to 0.5 (max) → ro1 × (0.5/0.15) = ×3.33  
- XM3: L_min=0.25, current L=0.75 → can ↑L to 2.0 → ro3 × (2.0/0.75)=×2.67  
- XM5: L_min=0.5, current L=0.5 → can ↑L to 1.5 → ro5 ×3.0  

All have room. But XM1 L↑ also ↓GBW (↑Cgs, ↑CL), yet GBW=66.4≫20 → acceptable. And XM1 L↑ has minimal impact on W (XM1 W=25.45µm <<1000). So **XM1 L↑ is safe & high-leverage for Gain**.

Compute Gain sensitivity to XM1 L:  
From iter11→12: XM1 L unchanged (0.15), so no data. But theory: Gain ∝ L1 (since ro1 ∝ L1, gm1 constant if gm_id fixed). So S_Gain,L1 ≈ +1.0 dB per +0.1 µm? Let's calibrate:  
If L1: 0.15→0.5 (+0.35), ro1 ×2.33 → Gain ×2.33 → +7.3 dB (20·log10(2.33)). So **ΔL1=+0.35 → +7.3 dB Gain**. We need +28 dB → would require ×25× gain → impossible. So ro scaling alone insufficient. Must also ↑gm_id of load/cascode to boost their ro? No — ro ∝ 1/ID × L, independent of gm_id. gm_id only sets bias point.

Wait: ro = VA / ID, VA ∝ L → ro ∝ L/ID. So yes, only L and ID matter. ID is fixed by Itail. So only way to ↑ro is ↑L.

Therefore, to lift Gain from 37 → 65 (+28 dB = ×25×), need total ro product ↑25×. But ro1×ro3×ro5 appears in parallel: 1/ro_total = 1/ro1 + 1/ro3 + 1/ro5 → dominated by smallest ro. Currently ro5 is smallest (XM5 L=0.5, same as XM1), but XM5 W=1000 → huge Cdb → may dominate CL, but for DC gain, ro5 matters.

So we must ↑L of **all low-L devices**: XM1, XM3, XM5, XM7.

But XM7 is at L_max=2.0 → cannot ↑. So focus on XM1, XM3, XM5.

Let’s tabulate current L and max L:

| Tube | curr L | max L | headroom |
|------|--------|--------|----------|
| XM1  | 0.15   | 0.50   | +0.35    |
| XM2  | 0.15   | 0.50   | +0.35    |
| XM3  | 0.75   | 2.00   | +1.25    |
| XM4  | 0.75   | 2.00   | +1.25    |
| XM5  | 0.50   | 1.50   | +1.00    |
| XM6  | 0.50   | 1.50   | +1.00    |
| XM7  | 2.00   | 2.00   | 0        |
| XM8  | 2.00   | 2.00   | 0        |
| XM9  | 0.50   | 2.00   | +1.50    |

XM9 L can also ↑ to boost ro9, improving tail node impedance → helps Gain slightly.

Now check W violation status (critical):

- XM5, XM6, XM7, XM8 all at W=1000 µm → **4 tubes at physical crash point**  
→ Per safety rule: must ↓W via ↑L and/or ↑gm_id  
→ XM5/XM6: gm_id already at max (18.0), so ↑L to 1.5  
→ XM7/XM8: gm_id=20.0 (max), L=2.0 (max) → **no maneuver room!** → This is a design flaw. Boundary escape rule says: when PM-control cascode hits both bounds, shift to second path — but here Gain is the issue, not PM. However, XM7/XM8 being saturated means they contribute minimal ro, dragging Gain down. So we must *accept* that XM7/XM8 cannot be improved, and overcompensate elsewhere.

But wait: XM7/XM8 are in the tail branch — their ro appears in series with ro9, affecting output impedance less than XM3/XM5. So priority remains XM1/XM3/XM5.

Also, XM9: current L=0.5, W=768.34 <1000, but close. Can ↑L to 1.0 or 1.5 to ↓W and ↑ro9.

Compute XM9 W reduction:  
curr idW = 0.299866, ID=230.4 → W=768.34  
target W≤800 is fine, but to create margin, aim W≤600 → need idW ≥ 230.4/600 = 0.384  
curr idW=0.2999 → need +28% idW → achievable by ↑L (since gm_id fixed at 22.0, max). L=0.5→1.0 often gives +30% idW → do L=1.0.

---

### **2. Boundary Check: XM7/XM8 at L=2.0 & gm_id=20.0 → both boundaries hit → trigger second path**

Second path per Rule #2:  
a) ↓L of input pair (XM1/XM2) to ↓parasitic capacitance → ↑ωₚ₂ → ↑PM  
b) ↓gm_id of XM1/XM2 to ↓GBW and ↑PM  

But current PM=73.3° > 60°, and GBW=66.4≫20, so (b) is viable **only if it helps Gain** — but ↓gm_id1 ↓gm1 → ↓Gain, which is opposite of need. So (b) is forbidden.

(a) ↓L of XM1/XM2? Current L=0.15=min → cannot ↓. So second path **not applicable** — instead, we face a *hard constraint*: XM7/XM8 cannot be tuned, so we must fix Gain by boosting other ro’s.

Thus, **first path remains: maximize L of all possible transistors**.

---

### **3. Gap补足：Gain needs +27.995 dB**

We use ro-scaling model:  
Gain ∝ (ro1 ∥ ro3 ∥ ro5 ∥ ro7 ∥ ro9)  
Dominant ro is min among them. From W and L, estimate relative ro:

ro ∝ L / (ID × k) where k depends on process. Since ID same for all except XM9 (2×), ro9 ∝ L9 / (2·ID)

Assume baseline ro at L=0.5: ro₀  
Then:
- XM1: L=0.15 → ro = 0.3 ro₀  
- XM3: L=0.75 → ro = 1.5 ro₀  
- XM5: L=0.5 → ro = 1.0 ro₀  
- XM7: L=2.0 → ro = 4.0 ro₀  
- XM9: L=0.5 → ro = 0.5 ro₀ (since ID double)  

So current min ro is XM1 → dominates. Thus, ↑L1 is highest ROI.

Set XM1 L = 0.5 (max) → ro1 × (0.5/0.15) = ×3.33 → Gain ×3.33 → +10.4 dB  
Similarly, XM3 L=0.75→2.0 → ×2.67 → +8.5 dB  
XM5 L=0.5→1.5 → ×3.0 → +9.5 dB  
XM9 L=0.5→1.0 → ×2.0 → +6.0 dB (but ro9 is in series with ro7, not parallel with output — less impact)  

Conservative total Gain boost: 10.4 + 8.5 + 9.5 = **+28.4 dB** → meets +27.995 gap.

So plan:
- XM1 L: 0.15 → 0.50  
- XM3 L: 0.75 → 2.00  
- XM4 L: 0.75 → 2.00  
- XM5 L: 0.50 → 1.50  
- XM6 L: 0.50 → 1.50  
- XM9 L: 0.50 → 1.00  

All within bounds.

Now gm_id adjustments:  
- XM1/XM2 gm_id was 18.0 → high, but ↑L allows ↓gm_id to reduce W and power, while maintaining gm1 = gm_id × ID. Since ID fixed, gm1 ∝ gm_id. But Gain ∝ gm1 × ro1, and ro1 ∝ L1, so if we ↑L1 and ↓gm_id1 such that gm1 constant, Gain unchanged but power ↓. However, gm_id ↓ → idW ↑ → W ↓ (good, XM1 W=25.45 is fine, but let’s keep for now). Actually, no need to change gm_id1 — keep at 18.0.

- XM3/XM4: current gm_id=20.0 (max), L↑ will ↓gm_id requirement? No — gm_id is bias point. Keep gm_id=20.0.

- XM5/XM6: gm_id=18.0 (max), keep.

- XM7/XM8: at bounds, keep.

- XM9: gm_id=22.0 (max), keep.

But wait: XM5/XM6 W=1000 at gm_id=18.0, L=0.5. At L=1.5, idW increases → W decreases. So safe.

Also, verify IDC: all IDs unchanged → Itail=230.4 µA → IDC=230 µA < 600 → OK.

SR=127.8 > 80 → OK.  
PM=73.3 > 60 → OK.  
GBW=66.4 > 20 → OK.  
Only Gain fails → this plan fixes it.

---

### **4. Final Parameter Decisions (iter13)**

| Tube | L (µm) | gm_id | Rationale |
|------|--------|--------|-----------|
| XM1  | **0.50** | 18.0 | ↑L to max to boost ro1 → +10.4 dB Gain; W=25.45→? (idW↑ slightly, but negligible) |
| XM2  | **0.50** | 18.0 | Match XM1 for symmetry |
| XM3  | **2.00** | 20.0 | ↑L to max to boost ro3 → +8.5 dB Gain; L_max=2.0, gm_id_max=20.0 → OK |
| XM4  | **2.00** | 20.0 | Same as XM3 |
| XM5  | **1.50** | 18.0 | ↑L to max to boost ro5 & ↓W (from 1000→~840); gm_id at max, cannot ↑ |
| XM6  | **1.50** | 18.0 | Same as XM5 |
| XM7  | 2.00 | 20.0 | At both boundaries; no change possible — accept as limiting factor |
| XM8  | 2.00 | 20.0 | Same |
| XM9  | **1.00** | 22.0 | ↑L from 0.5 to 1.0 to ↓W (768→~600) and ↑ro9; gm_id at max |

All L changes are monotonic (no oscillation), satisfying convergence damping.

No tube exceeds W=1000 after update:  
- XM5/XM6: L↑ → idW↑ → W↓ from 1000 to ~840  
- XM7/XM8: stuck at 1000, but no further degradation → acceptable for one iteration; next round may require topology change if Gain still short  
- XM1/XM2 W remains ~25 µm  
- XM3/XM4: L↑, gm_id same → idW same? No: idW depends on VGS−VT; ↑L at same gm_id requires ↑VGS−VT → ↑idW → W↓ from 167→? say 120  
- XM9: L↑ → idW↑ → W↓ from 768→600  

All safe.
